# LLP 1109.005: Mobile composer and transcript ownership

**Type:** Decision
**Status:** Draft
**Systems:** T3 Code mobile, Contract, TypeScript client, UIKit
**Author:** Nate's implementation session
**Date:** 2026-10-07
**Implementer:** T3 Code iOS implementation session, M4/M5
**Related:** LLP 1109, LLP 1109.000, LLP 1109.002, LLP 1109.003, LLP 1109.004

## Context

[confirmed] The supplied handoff requires pinned mobile UI365aa87982 over the existing desktop clone's protocol/client layer. The user authorized implementation decisions and diaries. This document records preparatory M4/M5 work; it does not accept native screen parity or mark those milestones complete.

[observed] Shared source887b249 owns V2 events, scoped drafts, provider locks, model-option memory, pending-write reconciliation and launch payloads. Mobile's ThreadSettingsSheet has a separate transient model choice: picking a row previews its options; Save applies it; Cancel abandons it. The mobile project/environment and branch pickers present live catalogs and may perform a real checkout before a launch.

## Retained model availability

[observed] The restored Moonbase seed selects codex/gpt-5.4. Its live server reports Codex enabled, installed, authenticated and ready, with seven newer advertised models and no gpt-5.4 row. The actual pinned mobile modelOptions.ts predicate reports that selection available; its unavailable notice applies only to Antigravity. The former mobile projection used the desktop catalog-membership gate for every provider, so it showed the wrong notice and disabled Send. The copied desktop sender and app-local queued editor also refused the retained selection.

[confirmed] On 2026-10-07, the parent implementation session approved the bounded app-local adaptation for this source mismatch. [model-availability.ts](../examples/t3-code/mobile/model-availability.ts) reproduces the pinned Antigravity predicate, including the configured driver fallback when a provider is absent. Thread admission, queued editing and the copied mobile sender use it while retaining the existing provider authentication/readiness, permission, runtime and ownership gates. No catalog model or provider metadata is fabricated.

[observed] A follow-up regression exposed saved options disappearing from that newly admitted path. The copied desktop dispatchSelection normalizes through catalog descriptors, which do not exist for the retained model. Pinned mobile thread-outbox-model.ts preserves the captured modelSelection. With parent approval, [model-send-selection.ts](../examples/t3-code/mobile/model-send-selection.ts) preserves captured options for an absent non-Antigravity model and delegates known models to the unchanged normalizer. The copied sender has mobile model admission and dispatch imports. The later clone subscription policy below is another explicit mobile-copy adaptation; root desktop code is untouched.

[observed] The focused suite passes 71 tests with 1,181 assertions after the retained-options correction in its second round. Mocked native transport exercises actual follow-up and new-task dispatch with saved options, successful launch adoption, known-model option filtering and the implicit Fast-mode default. The queued-edit journal saves the retained selection. Antigravity and lost provider authentication refuse before attachment reads or uploads; existing stale-owner tests pass. The copy audit compares all 297 bodies and permits only the explicit sender imports and guard adaptation. Strict TypeScript passes, and 32 cases match the actual pinned mobile unavailable function. A repeated read-only server projection yields modelUnavailable false and canSend true for the same retained selection and a local diagnostic draft. Independent review reran the 71-test suite and found no remaining source issue; its 104-case comparison also matched the pinned unavailable predicate.

[observed] The seeded server uses real installed providers. Its showcase setup substitutes host-label commands and a shell, but no Codex binary or provider catalog. The current CLI resolves to codex-cli 0.161.0, and the live provider is authenticated. No message was sent to that real provider during this investigation. Logs and sanitized before/after evidence are .context/t3-code-ios/model-availability-*. Native UI admission and real server outcomes remain to be exercised by the integrated app.

## Transcript ownership

[inferred] [thread.ts](../examples/t3-code/mobile/thread.ts) projects shared transcriptRows and request/composer state into strict mobile response shapes. It preserves shared row identifiers and disclosure operations. A supplied wall clock owns durations. Awaited mobileThreadPrepare answers perform shared detail/media/worktree reads; no native answer handle or autonomous timer survives the answer.

[observed] [thread.contract](../examples/t3-code/mobile/thread.contract) exposes a route scroller separately from [thread-composer.contract](../examples/t3-code/mobile/thread-composer.contract). Root owns navigation, keyboard/safe-area measurements and modal routes. The composer reports focus through an action prop. Prose currently uses Exact's portable Markdown reader; code cards use the existing TextMate grammar/tokenizer with pinned mobile GitHub theme selectors in [thread-highlight.ts](../examples/t3-code/mobile/thread-highlight.ts). The theme data retains its MIT notice.

[observed] Eight token fixtures match actual pinned Shiki4.2.0 under required upstream Node24.13.1 and Exact's pinned Bun1.4.2. TypeScript/JSON/bash/YAML each have light/dark fixtures. An initial unpinned Bun1.2.22 oracle produced incorrect comment colors; it was replaced by the actual Node24.13.1 oracle, retaining the comment fixture. These bounded tests do not prove all grammars or native rendering.

## Settings ownership

[inferred] [composer-settings.ts](../examples/t3-code/mobile/composer-settings.ts) owns only sheet presentation state: pending model/options, provider disclosure overrides, busy/error state and the sheet's draft/generation identity. Search/filter/legacy visibility are root inputs. Server config remains the catalog authority; shared provider availability/locks govern mutations. Mobile-specific catalog grouping, favorites-first stable ordering, simple visible-text search and hidden prompt-injected effort choices follow the pinned mobile source.

[observed] Save validates current catalog membership, permissions and the sheet's original applied selection. It places staged options into existing shared option memory and invokes the ordinary model command, whose single model-selection payload includes all options. A running turn uses shared next-turn staging. Cancel discards the pending sheet choice. Options for the already-applied model and runtime choices use existing commands immediately, matching the source's distinction. If the newly selected provider cannot support the current runtime, a compatible runtime command follows; those two server operations are not atomic, and a refusal leaves the sheet open with its error.

[observed] Favorites and model-option memory continue using shared local preferences. The unified mobile preference file supplies follow-up behavior before the shared send operation. [composer-behavior.ts](../examples/t3-code/mobile/composer-behavior.ts) reapplies that mobile setting after shared device presentation loads desktop preferences, and passes the actual primary/alternate intent through the existing composerSendIntent seam. Providers that cannot steer use queue. The shared dispatcher still owns payloads, permissions, pending operations and results. Environment, origin, project, thread, draft, generation, thread epoch and model/runtime identity are checked after preferences and before/after each native await preceding the first orchestration write. Once that write begins, shared pending reconciliation owns its outcome and a successful launch may select its new thread. An independent reproduction of a selection change during devicePresentation now dispatches nothing and preserves both drafts.

[observed] The stable composer textarea binds the pinned hardware-key chords through existing Contract keydown events. Enter/Meta+Enter follow the mobile Enter preference; alternate send uses Meta+Enter or Shift+Meta+Enter. Software Return remains a newline. Seven tests cover all queue/steer and primary/alternate combinations, unsupported steering, owner changes and desktop keybinding independence. Strict TypeScript passes. Hardware keyboard interaction, iPad command titles, long-press follow-up menus, haptics and rich input still require implementation or native evidence.

## New task ownership

### Incoming share inbox

[observed] [T3MobileIncomingShares.swift](../examples/t3-code/mobile/modules/apple/T3MobileIncomingShares.swift)
implements native ingress over the pinned mobile sharing transaction. Ordered
share type, MIME and value fields produce the source's SHA-256 identity. Accepted
files are copied into an app-owned inbox; byte copies, entry and parent directory
are synchronized before source cleanup and native acknowledgement. Replayed
acknowledgements retain the entry's instance ID and verify its byte references.
A later identical handoff receives a new instance ID. Text/URL normalization,
image/file classification, names, count and byte limits follow the source. Images
retain their original bytes. Results expose IDs and metadata, never paths.
Cleanup is restricted to owned roots and resolves traversal, symlinks and private
aliases; sender-owned files remain untouched.

[observed] [T3MobileIncomingShareTransfer.swift](../examples/t3-code/mobile/modules/apple/T3MobileIncomingShareTransfer.swift)
runs under the existing queued-edit preference/attachment mutex. A reservation
captures the exact draft, environment, project and origin; staging freezes the
selected attachment IDs and copies bytes into the existing composer stores.
Reservations protect staged bytes from cleanup. An immutable receipt is written
with the draft's text, attachments and mixed order before inbox consumption.
Native validation rejects lost receipts, changed destinations or missing adoption
evidence. Consumed receipts permit later draft edits. A later identical share is
a no-op only when an earlier consumed native receipt for that share and draft
matches the saved preferences, retaining the pinned per-draft deduplication.

[observed] [incoming-share-adoption.ts](../examples/t3-code/mobile/incoming-share-adoption.ts)
uses the existing draft-transfer lease and checks route/draft/generation ownership
after each await. Generic files wait for actual server capabilities. Existing
images/files and their order survive import; unsupported files produce warnings.
Lost persistence/consume replies retry the saved receipt without appending content
again. Release refuses a saved adoption and otherwise uses the existing guarded
attachment cleanup. The module dispatches this work off the UI thread without
retaining an Exact reply after completion.

[observed] Actual Foundation checks pass for ingress (36) and transfer (34),
including duplicate no-op consumption after the user edits away the original
text and refusal of unconsumed, missing, wrong-draft or forged prior evidence.
Nine TypeScript tests use the actual draft client/persistence with a mocked native
boundary; 95 assertions cover metadata, ordering, capability changes, ownership
changes and lost replies. The full mobile suite passes 1,431 tests. These separate
tests do not establish an end-to-end system sharing flow.

[observed] [incoming-share-inbox.ts](../examples/t3-code/mobile/incoming-share-inbox.ts)
now owns the native inbox watch and pinned presentation transition. Root commits
its presentation state and route together, rejecting stale route/state/inbox
revisions. Any mounted new-task flow defers presentation; dismissal suppresses
reopening that share on foreground refresh. Project choice carries the share ID;
a reservation resumes its exact saved draft, and conflicting routes redirect
before project or branch changes. Import blocks composer actions until it settles.

[observed] Cancellation captures a durable pre-import projection at reservation
and an immutable adoption projection before the first receipt-bearing preference
write. Native compares the caller and disk projections, journals a cancelling
intent, restores only that draft with a higher revision, and removes its receipt.
Later edits and completed consumption refuse rollback. TypeScript uses the existing
draft-transfer lease and local compare-and-swap restore. Retry/Cancel import alerts
follow the pinned screen. Empty drafts retain metadata during reservation/adoption;
cold hydration preserves explicit null model choices.

[observed] The full mobile suite passes 1,457 tests / 10,258 assertions, including
inbox races, reservation redirects and cancellation ownership. Strict production
TypeScript, repository build/test/Clippy/formatting/boot, app build/Clippy and fresh
iPhone/iPad builds pass. Both devices import an actual Foundation-created text
inbox item through project selection and persist its consumed native receipt.
The phone reopens the saved draft after restart and suppresses a dismissed inbox
item on foreground return. Isolated fixtures are removed afterward. Viewed
screenshots and disk evidence are under the ignored incoming-share-ui folder.

[observed] Native cancellation helpers pass 39 checks and existing transfer checks
pass 34. Coordinator compilation and rollback pass, but the temporary cleanup
fixture omits required registry fields; ownership quarantine retains its bytes.
The coordinator cleanup assertion is unresolved at the three-round boundary,
and a fresh allowance has been requested. Cancellation device acceptance is not
claimed. These changes remain under validation.

[observed] Native reads still report `available: false`. GAP006 prevents an
embedded Share Extension and App Group producer. The device drive explicitly
seeds the real local ingress; it does not claim a system share-sheet handoff.
Ingress/adoption never submits a turn. OS handoff, complete appearance/motion
parity and release performance remain open.

[decision] Standalone NewTaskFile links grant fileReady without draft ready. The flow
retains its previous selection for Back, but the file visit cannot prepare or mutate that
draft. Explicit environment/cwd reads use their own endpoint identity, as described in
[LLP1109.006](1109.006-review-and-files.decision.md#standalone-workspace-files).

[inferred] [new-task.ts](../examples/t3-code/mobile/new-task.ts) projects synchronized focused/fleet shells and uses JSON-encoded environment/project identity pairs for project picks. Selected project drafts remain in shared environment/project slots. [draft.ts](../examples/t3-code/mobile/draft.ts) calls the shared composerOps draft reducer synchronously before its first await, then persists through the existing serialized native queue. The full shared command path awaits presentation before reduction and can cross project ownership; mobile bypasses that preamble for text input. A delayed-input/project-switch regression covers this boundary. Shared environment-run-on moves the same logical project's draft between machines. A project on another live environment connects and synchronizes that actual environment before opening its project draft.

[observed] [draft-attachment-order.ts](../examples/t3-code/mobile/draft-attachment-order.ts) retains one local-ID order per draft key across the shared image and file stores. Picker acceptance records the original mixed order before native editor insertion can await or persist. The attachment strip and independent-draft projection consume that order. Persistence filters absent owners without pruning the live order, so a failed image removal can restore its original position. Hydration uses the same preference document; recovered queued edits supply their existing ordered attachment array. Old records without order retain their existing grouped order because past chronology cannot be recovered.

[observed] Fresh shared Send payloads map each outgoing occurrence through the shared sender's snapshot and captured file-reference order, then restore draft chronology. This preserves distinct entries even when local files share one uploaded asset ID and the prompt references are rearranged. Existing pending retries keep their captured payload unchanged. The native editor-insertion fallback also names the captured draft key for ordinary threads; opening another thread during insertion cannot append the old attachment reference to the new draft. Nine attachment-order tests and an independent actual-write comparison cover these boundaries; this does not activate outbox delivery.

[observed] Branch resources read the authenticated session and actual VCS catalog. Checkout permission uses explicit permissions when present, including an empty list. A worktree base selection records shared draft context; checking out another local branch uses the real shared VCS command. Selecting the current branch requires no redundant checkout. Shared branch paging receives the actual Contract reachend event and performs its pending next-page read within the awaited root answer.

[inferred] New task Send invokes the existing shared send/launch path. The shared client allocates identifiers, persists pending operations, checks draft/model ownership before dispatch and reconciles uncertain writes. The adapter returns submitted=true only after a new actual thread identity is available for the same environment/project/generation. Root navigates on that result, never on a tap or predicted thread id. An uncertain launch retains the shared pending entry and original draft.

[observed] The new `/new` route is a project chooser. [new-task-flow.ts](../examples/t3-code/mobile/new-task-flow.ts) owns a containing route session and current visit; it does not adopt an unrelated existing composer selection. The chooser reuses synchronized Home project grouping and source search over real member paths. `/new/draft` and its environment, branch, settings and attachment children become ready only after explicit project selection or a valid environment/project query. Catalog readiness comes from the saved enabled catalog, preference hydration and focused/fleet loading state; an empty settled catalog is valid. Invalid identities return to the chooser without selecting a fallback. Each visit applies its explicit query once, while a changed URL on that visit invalidates the prior application. Returning from a child preserves subsequent user choices.

[observed] A branch query performs the real authenticated `vcs.switchRef` before applying its returned branch identity. An existing worktree path follows the pinned source and bypasses checkout. An already-dispatched checkout blocks another preparation until it settles. Route, selection and generation checks prevent its late result from changing another draft. Root model, attachment, voice Start, document and branch producers require the current ready flow owner; existing voice cleanup retains its captured owner. A direct model child stages an actual settings session before becoming ready. Project selection invokes the shared thread reducer synchronously before its first await, and validates the resulting selection again after persistence. Tests cover departure during unsubscribe/checkout, same-route external selection, overlapping text persistence and an explicit unprepared identity denying access to the prior draft.

[observed] This remains a partial adaptation of pinned NewTaskFlowProvider: Exact retains one shared draft slot per environment/project instead of a fresh independent draft identity per flow. Revisiting a project restores that slot, preserving existing text rather than overwriting it with another project's draft. Bare draft links require project choice even when one project exists. Saved draft/outbox/share identities are refused explicitly; the later clone slice admits cloning=1; incoming share ingestion and source project favicon presentation remain incomplete. The later scratch slice below adds actual no-project creation without claiming independent per-flow drafts. The chooser uses real grouped rows but does not establish native geometry parity. A new isolated chooser/flow Contract fixture passes after one manifest correction; the combined root and native targets remain stopped at their existing retry boundaries. Strict root TypeScript and 26 focused tests with 120 assertions pass for this slice.

### Add Project and tracked clones

[observed] Add Project handled controls use `retainFocus=true`, matching the pinned ScrollView `keyboardShouldPersistTaps="handled"` policy. The primary, shared row and publish buttons retain focus through press dispatch; unhandled scroll content remains outside that policy. Native phone validation advances Git URL to destination on one keyboard-visible tap, changes the folder on one tap while retaining the same editor, and dismisses editing on unhandled content. Evidence is `.context/t3-code-ios/add-project-keyboard/README.md`. This is an app translation using an existing public property, not a framework workaround.

[observed] The five source routes are Add Project, Repository, Clone destination, Local folder and New project. [add-project-path.ts](../examples/t3-code/mobile/add-project-path.ts) ports mobile path, provider and project-payload rules and reuses existing shared path classifiers. An independent differential run compared 2,243 inputs with the pinned upstream helpers. [add-project.ts](../examples/t3-code/mobile/add-project.ts) retains serializable input per containing sheet and route visit. Each native answer rechecks environment, generation, live permission and request identity. Folder selection invalidates an older prepare answer. Local typing causes no catalog or auth reads; external revision changes still invalidate preparation.

[observed] Root owns preparation, mutation replies, native alerts and the literal 15-second project-arrival deadline. Only an actual shared shell project admits draft navigation. A transport-uncertain creation refuses a second create; optional publication failure retains the acknowledged project and displays its notice before navigation. Alerts are consumed once. Source publication starts concurrently with project selection, while this implementation waits for publication before navigation. This timing difference and live publication verification remain open. No personal provider repository was published for validation.

[observed] [new-task-clone.ts](../examples/t3-code/mobile/new-task-clone.ts) reads the existing clone subscription. Mobile snapshot initializes that shared subscription after refresh; the existing generation registry prevents duplicates and the next refresh drains events. A known initial cloning=1 draft blocks Send until its first authoritative answer. A running, failed or cancelled clone blocks actual Send as well as the button. An empty list, unsupported tracking or failed subscription releases that client-side block, matching pinned state/projectClones.ts. The mobile copy of [project-clones-live.ts](../examples/t3-code/mobile/shared/project-clones-live.ts) now ignores its retained list on stream error, so the shared sender agrees with the visible state. The retained stream data and desktop source remain unchanged. Actual sender regressions prove healthy clones refuse and failed subscriptions reach server admission.

[observed] [new-task-clone.contract](../examples/t3-code/mobile/new-task-clone.contract) places the banner above workspace controls. Cancel and Retry require fresh source-control:write; Remove uses non-force project.delete and orchestration:operate. Captured owner, route, project and generation prevent stale dispatch/navigation. Successful Remove replaces the containing sheet with Home, retaining any earlier Home/thread prefix. The actual pinned React Navigation router and Exact Runner both permit the resulting duplicate Home entries. Native phone validation created a disposable project, entered its ready draft, and rendered a real failed clone plus Retry from the seeded server. Native Remove exposed shared shell fallback racing the reply; the flow now holds its busy state until acknowledgment and permits Home only for the captured route/environment/generation with that project absent. Route departure still suppresses navigation. Native Remove passes its third round after explicit projection of the closed mutation result. The first two rounds deleted the server project but leaked the helper-only removed field, so the decoder refused navigation. Final state and a read-only database query prove Home replacement and deletion. Every provider's live integration, iPad/light/dark pixels and Release measurements remain required.

### Scratch tasks and queue boundaries

[observed] The bounded scratch slice follows pinned `NewTaskRouteScreen.tsx` and `NewTaskFlowProvider`: the chooser offers **No project** / **Start a task without a project**, or **Start without a project** when the project catalog is empty. [new-task-scratch.ts](../examples/t3-code/mobile/new-task-scratch.ts) chooses the selected eligible connected environment, otherwise the first connected eligible environment; cached reconnecting shells do not qualify. The action captures the actual origin/environment/generation/scratch root and current draft selection, rechecks `orchestration:operate` from the native session, and invokes unchanged shared `openScratchProject` / `openRemoteScratch`. Only the returned project ID after real shell adoption can be selected. Existing text in both the prior project and scratch draft remains intact. Root's existing flow result carries the requesting route and `/new/draft` destination; no synthetic project or second client is introduced.

[observed] Scratch context always uses local mode without a branch/worktree, including saved stale contexts, direct branch-bearing links, and send admission. The workspace controls are hidden and a scratch branch route redirects to its draft. [T3MobileScratchClock.swift](../examples/t3-code/mobile/modules/apple/T3MobileScratchClock.swift) supplies the unchanged helper's awaited `timelineSleep`; module dispatch also supplies its `r10Wake` notification. Waiters resolve once on delay or module teardown. The shared helper remains bounded to twenty 500 ms polls, plus request latency; this is not a claim that a slow shell request has a strict ten-second wall-clock deadline. Let-go remains superseded even though the shared optional-wait helper catches failures.

[observed] Scratch **Run on** uses the existing shared `runOnEnvironment` directly after the guarded destination check, avoiding the command preamble's load/presentation await. A narrow app-local journal captures the source/destination draft, context, balance and saved-selection cells before and immediately after the shared reduction. On failure it restores only cells still equal to that staged write in the same store, independently of the currently selected draft. Failed Connect replies are intercepted before the shared current-selection rollback; superseded replies keep their original failure kind. This corrects the independent-review witness where selecting another project during fleet stop stranded source text in the destination. It never connects after that cancellation and preserves differing later text/context/selection writes. Exact's existing per-project slot requires two temporary refusals: an occupied destination draft is preserved rather than overwritten; a source draft with attachments cannot switch machines because the shared move owns only text/context. Mobile's independent flow draft and attachment-transfer semantics remain open work.

[observed] The server queue is now an internal thread sheet. [queue.ts](../examples/t3-code/mobile/queue.ts) projects shared queueState and dispatches the real remove, reorder, steer and resume commands after a fresh operate-grant check. Action tickets capture the selected owner, route visit, queued run and displayed order. An initially empty sheet stays open; a visit that saw rows closes after its last row leaves. Root checks the returned visit and selected environment/thread before dismissing. The composer opens this sheet from its queued count and blurs its focused input before navigation. The count uses the source glass-fallback token; its combined floating-status capsule and glass animation remain incomplete.

[observed] [queue-read.ts](../examples/t3-code/mobile/queue-read.ts) prepares signed image thumbnails separately from the immediate queue snapshot. Root owns the preparation mutation and stable request key. The cache keys include attachment ID, filename, MIME type and disposition; it retains only the current visit's displayed images. Reads become stale after five minutes or earlier server expiry. Foreground preparation replaces the source's autonomous thirty-minute refresh and one-hour idle cache. Every worker settles before the answer returns, including superseded siblings. The view shows up to three images plus an attachment count, actual queue errors, held-queue resume and context actions. Its header uses the existing native title projection; bottom padding follows max(safeBottom,16)+8. The current medium/large sheet detents differ from the source fractions0.65/0.95. Swipe Remove, drag/reorder motion, native menu geometry and runtime presentation remain unfinished.

[observed] The queued-edit implementation uses [queued-edit-state.ts](../examples/t3-code/mobile/queued-edit-state.ts) for a unique in-memory session and dedicated draft key. The copied shared queued-edit helper borrows the ordinary draft, and its pending completion can clear ordinary text and attachment bytes even when an editor uses a separate key. An executed dispatch witness demonstrated that failure. [composer-target.ts](../examples/t3-code/mobile/composer-target.ts) therefore carries the content owner through keyboard input, voice, review, terminal and media callbacks. Model/runtime settings stay on the actual thread. Cancelled or replaced sessions never resolve as ordinary drafts.

[inferred] [queued-edit.ts](../examples/t3-code/mobile/queued-edit.ts) prepares ordered retained and new attachments, checks current grants and reserves an immutable queued-run.edit command through the app-local native owner. [T3MobileQueuedEdit.swift](../examples/t3-code/mobile/modules/apple/T3MobileQueuedEdit.swift) persists editor revisions, reservations, issued commands and acknowledgments. The same coordinator covers foreground and fleet transports. It gates the actual durable RPC methods and conflicting shared pending writes per environment. A retry uses the saved command ID and payload. Ordinary drafts and unrelated environments retain their own owners. Cleanup transfers byte releases durably before unlinking; unresolved issued commands cannot be discarded by Cancel. These source decisions remain under integration review and are not runtime parity evidence.

[inferred] Run-loss recovery carries text, explicit context and newly picked attachments into an empty ordinary draft; retained server attachments are excluded. A persisted mobile recovery marker prevents duplicate adoption. [mobile-draft-recovery.ts](../examples/t3-code/mobile/mobile-draft-recovery.ts) restores supported non-PNG image metadata at the shared loader's first presentation boundary and extends a new ordinary command's context before shared pending persistence. The shared loader itself accepts only PNG snapshots. The app has one MobileDraftClient subclass using the unchanged shared reducer; it does not swap ordinary slots or route queued saves through shared finishPending.

[observed] The durable offline outbox remains absent. It needs per-message records, revision/CAS edits and removals, editing locks, capability-gated draining, retry policy and attachment cleanup. The native queued-edit journal handles only this existing server-message operation; it is not that outbox.

[observed] The queue suite passes13 tests with87 assertions. Independent review executes17 tests with94 assertions, including separate foreign-pending, worker-lifetime, duplicate-signing and selection-change witnesses. The queue view and count trigger compile in a new isolated Contract fixture. Aggregate mobile tests pass473 tests with2788 assertions; strict root TypeScript,398 captured modules with no ambient refusals, formatting and boot checks pass. The root remains source-reviewed only. No paused root/composer/header/native target was retried, and no queue control has UIKit or pixel-parity evidence.

[observed] Twenty-eight focused new-task tests (157 assertions) pass on pinned Bun1.4.2, including real shared scratch RPC/shell decoding, permission denial, remote generation routing, stale project/route refusal, lost polling answers, direct-link context normalization, destination preservation, captured-slot rollback after project changes, and concurrent edits during refused/superseded moves. Strict TypeScript, the existing isolated chooser Contract fixture, and integrated actual Exact API Swift typechecking pass. A native Foundation harness executes the actual scratch clock body and verifies delay, invalid-duration refusal and exactly-once teardown. No full native/root target was retried and no UIKit interaction parity is claimed. Evidence is recorded in `.context/t3-code-ios/m5-scratch-progress.md`.

## Root seam

[observed] [composer-shapes.contract](../examples/t3-code/mobile/composer-shapes.contract) declares ComposerSettingsSnapshot, ComposerActionResult, NewTaskSnapshot, NewTaskResult, NewTaskPreparation and ComposerColors. Model/settings and new-task components consume those shapes, and delegate headers/search, routing, attachments and settings-page presentation to root. NewTaskResult returns environmentId,projectId,threadId and submitted. Root scopes branch reads to route, query, readiness and draft owner changes, rather than each keystroke. Thread selection replies carry their requesting route identity and cannot navigate over a newer route. The shared client remains the sole protocol mutation owner; test-native replies appear only in unit fixtures.

## Alternatives and consequences

[inferred] A second mobile client/reducer would split draft/submission ownership. Using desktop picker rendering directly would lose the source's staged Save behavior and catalog presentation. The selected approach keeps shared protocol ownership and app-local mobile presentation, at the cost of explicit adapters and some shared desktop constraints (for example, stricter writable admission).

[observed] The checkpoint still lacks full native parity evidence, rich composer chips/selection, picker search toolbar/filter header integration, native media/voice, offline outbox, exact provider-unavailable/default retention behavior, source-specific tool rendering, and large-catalog virtualization. The latter is currently ordinary scrolling: Exact's virtualized list requires one direct each, while these preparation components include header content. No framework workaround was added. Root integration is written but its combined Contract compile is paused at the retry decision. Native validation remains required.

[observed] Focused tests exercise staged Save/Cancel, stale catalog/permission refusal, shared option payloads, project draft isolation, branch permissions, ownership changes before dispatch, confirmed launch identity and uncertain launch retention. Contract checks reached the three-round stop with a final numeric-text conversion correction awaiting the parent’s permitted next validation. Strict TypeScript passes. Validation state must be updated only from the actual integrated checks.

## Attachment preparation

[observed] The app-native Photo Library and Choose Files adapters feed the shared attachment reducer and upload paths. Supported image bytes keep their actual MIME type; other picked images use a bounded JPEG conversion. Native upload normalization supplies MIME type when the desktop image path omits it. The root renders staged thumbnail/removal entries and accepts image-only sends. This preparation does not yet match file icons, preview/share transitions, eager upload progress, rich inline chips or voice input. Native picker interaction remains unverified.

## Media presentation

[observed] [media-preview.ts](../examples/t3-code/mobile/media-preview.ts) resolves only an attachment present in the selected shared transcript or current composer draft. Transcript previews reauthorize through the existing `assets.createUrl` RPC when opened, then retain that first signed URL for the route instance. A changed environment/thread/generation refuses late authorization. Composer previews pass an owned draft UUID; filesystem paths and bytes stay below the native seam. Share repeats the ownership check before presenting. Failed native decoding can invalidate the presentation cache before an explicit retry.

[observed] Pinned mobile iOS uses Quick Look for native image/document previews and AVKit for videos. The app-local `T3MobileMedia*` files adapt those pinned implementations with existing native-view lifetime hooks. Quick Look owns zoom, document controls and its share action. AVKit owns playback controls, pauses on background, and restores the preceding audio-session category when still applicable. Native file/share operations lease original-byte copies; removing a draft does not delete an already-open lease. A separate share uses UIActivityViewController and retains its own copy through dismissal. Removing the presenter view cancels loading and dismisses its viewer; completion includes the request identity so root can reject late navigation.

[observed] The updated base 80eccbe's X29/#115 fix covers bundled PDF iframes. Its gap map still excludes app-file video/iframe and a dedicated PDF viewer; it does not replace these pinned mobile Quick Look/AVKit controls. This slice introduces no framework change or shared protocol fork. Source thumbnail transition anchors and iPad share-anchor geometry remain unimplemented; the system presentation fallback is used. Pinned AttachmentFileScreen's text/Markdown/table rendered/source modes remain separate from its native file-viewer action. Native interaction/parity has not been demonstrated.

[observed] The independent media Contract target passed its second round after adding the required node root. Strict production TypeScript passed; media Swift typechecking passed its second round after supplying the existing generated module key definitions omitted from the first command. Eight focused ownership tests and the current aggregate 101 tests passed under pinned Bun1.4.2. This did not retry the stopped combined composer/root Contract or full native build targets.

[observed] [attachment-document.ts](../examples/t3-code/mobile/attachment-document.ts)
now renders bounded text, Markdown, CSV and TSV separately from the native viewer.
The pinned file classifier, fatal UTF-8 decoder, delimited parser and rendered/source
selection are copied with provenance. Native reads stop after 1 MiB plus one byte,
even when a remote server ignores Range. The extra byte distinguishes a complete
file from a truncated preview. Tables retain the source's separate limits of 100
rows, 30 columns and 2,000 characters per cell. Text and table modes share one read;
changing appearance only recolors the cached lines. A late read cannot restore a
removed attachment, a replaced read, or another conversation's file.

[observed] [attachment-document-actions.ts](../examples/t3-code/mobile/attachment-document-actions.ts)
provides Preview/Source, word wrap, Copy contents/preview, Save or share, Open in
file viewer and Remove from draft. Share and explicit native viewer actions mint
a fresh server URL. The document keeps its original rendered HTML URL while a
source read older than five minutes reauthorizes. HTML uses an app-local WKWebView;
its per-document view identity rejects callbacks from replaced loads. Navigation
and main-frame HTTP errors retain a retryable document view. A native format that
Quick Look cannot show also retains a retryable fallback. Closing an explicitly
opened native viewer returns to the rendered document; closing a viewer that was
the whole visit returns to the conversation.

[observed] The document component compiled in isolation on its third round after
adding its native module names and correcting the fixture and unsupported

into an NSObject delegate. The independent source review found and checked fixes
for supplied-time use, viewer URL expiry, failure fallback, HTML error handling,

isolated-test evidence only.

text, one synchronized horizontal scroller for all source lines, upstream Markdown

[observed] [attachment-audio.contract](../examples/t3-code/mobile/attachment-audio.contract)
now provides the pinned inline audio controls, timestamp, loading state and retry.

[observed] [T3MobileAudioSession.swift](../examples/t3-code/mobile/modules/apple/T3MobileAudioSession.swift)
tracks native playback and recording owners before deactivating the shared OS audio

## Streaming haptics

[observed] [haptics.ts](../examples/t3-code/mobile/haptics.ts) reads the last actual streaming assistant descriptor before rendered rows omit empty text. The descriptor refuses an unloaded or mismatched environment/thread. [app.contract](../examples/t3-code/mobile/app.contract) keeps a baseline and throttle clock for each mounted thread route, removes that state when the route leaves, and dispatches the existing `haptic("selection")` command on a new assistant identity or text growth at least 320ms after the last pulse. Mount and owner-change hydration remain silent. The task key contains descriptor identity and text length, so elapsed time alone cannot produce feedback.

[observed] Nine Bun tests with 35 assertions cover the descriptor and transition. An isolated Contract plan containing the actual root transition block compiles and passes 11 checkpoints in the real Runner, including hydration, throttled growth, no periodic pulse, a new message, empty/restarted streaming and route cleanup/re-entry. An independent same-family session repeated the tests and Runner witness and compared the root block with the fixture. The combined root compiler and full native build were not retried.

[unknown] The shared client projects one selected thread. Retained routes do not independently subscribe to other threads, so returning to a retained thread can observe growth that occurred while another thread was selected. Physical-device feedback and native route timing remain unverified.

## Native Thread header

[observed] [T3MobileThreadHeader.swift](../examples/t3-code/mobile/modules/apple/T3MobileThreadHeader.swift) implements the pinned `useThreadHeaderOptions.tsx`, `ThreadGitControls.tsx` and native screens grouping patch through public `ExactRoute.controller.navigationItem`. Its zero-size, noninteractive configuration view owns no navigation stack, protocol state or answer handle. Root supplies actual title, project/environment subtitle, menu items, permissions, visit identity and menu version; native selections emit captured owner/routeKey/version/itemID for root validation.

[observed] The native-stack adapter reverses right items before UIKit grouping. Liquid Glass compact right controls therefore use Terminal, Files and Git in one fixed group. Split right controls use Terminal, Git and Files. Split left controls follow 18 points of spacing, with optional Return to chat, sidebar/maximize and New task. On iOS 26 and later, each side shares a group. Earlier iOS puts each control in its own fixed group, matching the pinned native screens patch. Compact history retains native Back; no-history uses Go to threads list. Liquid Glass uses the native subtitle, editor navigation-item style and17-point heavy title; earlier iOS uses the UIKit default title font and solid default material without a subtitle. The source explicitly leaves the Thread title style undefined outside Liquid Glass. The menus use source SF names and actual supplied labels/subtitles.

[observed] Callbacks require matching captured owner, visit and menu version, current enabled membership, focused configuration, a live route at its navigation controller's top and a mounted window. Ended/replaced routes and destroyed module/view owners retire callbacks. Repeated identical configurations retain their menu objects; native projection may reapply those objects after Exact's route update. No delegate or hierarchy is replaced.

[observed] The actual extracted configuration/eligibility body passes23 executable Foundation checks. An isolated actual-API Swift check passed on its second attempt after adding the omitted fixture module entry; the first failure was fixture-only. The combined iOS17 source roster and final focused source check pass. This evidence does not prove native title width, glass grouping, menu presentation, compact Back or split geometry. Root/data integration, source working-control access for Agents/Browser/Devices, and UIKit acceptance are tracked separately; no full native build was run for this slice.

[observed] Pre-Liquid-Glass compact uses the pinned fallback policy. `NativeHeaderToolbar.tsx` returns no view; its later layout effect replaces `unstable_headerRightItems`, rather than adding a second toolbar. At this call site `ThreadRouteScreen.tsx` sets direct Files only in split layout, and `deriveFileInspectorPaneLayout` also requires split layout, so the compact fallback has neither direct Files nor the auxiliary-pane toggle. Its declarative order is Terminal followed by Git controls. The same native-stack reversal produces separate native groups in Git, Terminal order. Terminal has no menu title; Git uses “Git controls” and contains branch/status, quick action, Review and More, without the declarative menu's Merge back item. [T3MobileThreadHeader.swift](../examples/t3-code/mobile/modules/apple/T3MobileThreadHeader.swift) now applies that menu policy only below iOS 26 in compact layout. The pinned `react-native-screens@4.28.0.patch`, lines 1094 to 1109, also leaves background sharing disabled before iOS 26. Both split sides therefore use separate fixed groups for every item, including Return to chat, sidebar/maximize and New task. The 18-point spacing item remains separate on every OS. Captured action guards are unchanged. Source `Stack.tsx` disables header shadow and uses default solid material before Liquid Glass.

[observed] The actual extracted configuration and presentation policy passes 45 executable Foundation checks. They cover action eligibility, compact and split menu contents, separate pre26 groups, optional Return to chat, shared iOS 26 groups and empty groups. Focused iOS 17 actual-API Swift typechecking passes on the second finishing attempt; the first omitted the existing generated data-key fixture. Evidence is in `.context/t3-code-ios/thread-header-native/pre26/finish-test-round1.log` and `pre26-finish-source-round2.log`. [observed] The restored upstream Release iPhone capture `.context/t3-code-ios/oracle-release-restored/iphone-6.9/light/thread.png` shows Terminal, Files, Git from left to right. The installed pinned `@react-navigation/native-stack/src/views/useHeaderConfigProps.tsx`, lines 383 to 385, reverses `rightItems` before `processBarButtonItems`. The screens patch then preserves that array while constructing `trailingItemGroups`. The native coordinator now performs this missing reversal before all trailing grouping, including the compact pre26 fallback. Leading items retain their declared order. The actual extracted policy passes 45 checks, and actual-API iOS 17 Swift typechecking passes on the first attempt. Logs are `thread-header-native/pre26/trailing-order-test-round1.log` and `thread-header-native/trailing-order-source-round1.log`. ## Thread header data and action ownership

[observed] The app-local [thread-header.ts](../examples/t3-code/mobile/thread-header.ts)
projects the selected shared thread/project, saved environment label, resolved
project scripts, shared terminal metadata and shared Git status into the native
configuration above. The action-sensitive menu version includes the script
recipe and merge source, rather than the ticking client revision. Its strict
Contract snapshot exposes no command recipe outside the configuration and no
native answer handle. Files, Review and More return real route locations without
network side effects. Compact/split special actions match native eligibility.

[observed] [thread-header-model.ts](../examples/t3-code/mobile/thread-header-model.ts)
preserves the pinned mobile quick-action function, including stacked commit/push/PR
choices. The additive Git adapter operation uses the existing shared action stream
and default-branch confirmation. Git status and refs authorize `orchestration:read`,
as declared by pinned server `RpcAuthorization.ts` for `subscribeVcsStatus`,
`vcs.refreshStatus` and `vcs.listRefs`; the former app-local
`source-control:read` check named a nonexistent scope and has been corrected.
Explicit server grant denial remains authoritative.

[observed] Merge back captures the actual source thread, target and completed run,
reauthorizes operation permission, and uses the shared durable dispatch path with
`creationSource: mobile`. Before issuance it rejects changed ownership, route,
run or competing pending writes. After issuance the immutable operation consumes
its acknowledgment and clears its captured environment's pending record even
when the route has departed. Presentation admission is checked separately, so a
late successful merge cannot navigate a different screen. Known-unsent route
changes clean only their own pending command; foreign pending records survive.

[observed] Terminal entries distinguish read and operate grants. Failed or ended
metadata streams hide stale session entries and use the source UUID allocation
fallback until fresh metadata arrives; preparation does not turn the retained
shared list into a fresh answer. A project script stages the actual command and
its detail-preferred worktree/project environment. Root runs the explicit launch
mutation before mounting the terminal view. The one-use launch is claimed before
its permission read, consumed before opening, and never automatically retried
after failed or uncertain writes. Native terminal mounting remains gated until
that mutation settles. This avoids competing first-open environment choices.

[observed] Focused header/Git tests pass37 tests with229 assertions. These cover
real standard read grants, stale menu recipes, native-free route projection,
default-branch confirmation, grant downgrade, route changes during authorization
and persistence, foreign pending preservation, acknowledged merge completion,
metadata invalidation, script working directory/environment and duplicate or
uncertain launch writes. Strict focused TypeScript passes. Two initial expectations
incorrectly expected swallowed supersession; they now assert the shared let-go
rejection. A subsequent concurrent-launch fixture omitted authenticated session
status; correcting that fixture completed the third test round. No extra native
build was run by the data author.

[observed] A subsequent peer finding showed that Exact replacement preserves
route IDs. Parent-authorized correction adds actual location and selected terminal
to the observed route identity. Each observed identity change retires the previous
observation, including away-and-back transitions. Launch awaits and menu actions
check that captured observation; a same-ID replacement during terminal.open cannot
inject the old script. The focused regression target passes on its first attempt;
the combined header/Git suites now pass38 tests with235 assertions, and focused
TypeScript passes. Parent supplies the new scalar through the existing options
argument; the native JSON schema is unchanged.

[observed] Native execution is recorded below. Unit results alone do not establish UIKit or visual parity. The pre26 fallback uses the native header policy described above.

The status cap subtracts those measured widths, the source 38.5-point arrow reservation and 32 points from the available chat width. ## Composer focus restoration

[observed] The pinned use-thread-settings-sheet-presentation hook captures editor focus, visible keyboard or an in-progress restoration before opening Model settings. Fresh source recordings also show focused Thread returning with its keyboard after Cancel and nested Runtime Back/Cancel, while an initially unfocused New Task remains unfocused.

[observed] Root now captures the exact route-specific HTML editor ID, composer content owner, presented sheet ID and focus intent before blurring. Intent includes the app's observed keyboardDocked fact, whose precise meaning is bottom keyboard-guide obstruction beyond the safe area, including a hardware accessory bar. It does not infer keyboard visibility from focus or viewport dimensions. The editor stays at its existing plan site. Focus and blur callbacks carry their editor ID and content owner, and root accepts only its active editor or the captured editor's blur while settings remains open. The stored focus identity also includes both values, so a retained route cannot leave a different route spuriously focused.

[observed] The pure return policy in [thread-composer.contract](../examples/t3-code/mobile/thread-composer.contract) rejects missing, unknown or replaced owners and missing or superseded routes. It retains expansion through the captured sheet and its nested Model routes. Returning to that same editor with a pending focus intent transitions once to restoring and issues the public focus command. An actual matching editor-focus callback closes the phase. An initially unfocused opening keeps expansion only through settings and makes no focus request on return. Reopening while restoring clears the host's previous pending focus and captures a fresh sheet. Invalidating an already-issued request sends blur for the exact editor, which also cancels the host's pending intent.

[observed] The initial implementation used a root task with the existing one-shot Contract scheduler after the derived phase changed. The bounded iPad correction below supersedes its single-command delivery. That tick is not a guess at UIKit animation duration. PresenterIOS queues the single focus command through its own navigation/modal lifecycle and validates the retained node, authored ID, active route, window attachment and positive bounds before delivery. No app retry timer or native focus shim was added. Model roots now request modal presentation with the public large detent. Compact New Task requests modal presentation with an initial large detent. The existing form-sheet data marker distinguishes composer and new-task for the parent-owned public UIKit sheet configuration; it keeps the earlier pairing marker. Numeric Contract detents are points and are not used as a fraction.

[observed] The ignored focus-restoration-witness copies five actual component/dependency files and extracts the production root focus declarations, observer, callback and opening action verbatim. Its Contract build passes round 3. Round 1 exposed that child components cannot own tasks; the observer moved to root. Round 2 reported only the fixture's unformatted boolean text. The real Runner compiles and executes on its first round. It observes actual emitted focus/blur commands, not a second implementation of the policy. It passes focused and nested return, initially unfocused New Task, captured keyboard-guide intent, changed/unknown owners, replacement between return and reconciliation, queued-focus cancellation, retained-route late callbacks, superseding and removed routes, reopening during restoration, and stable distinct editor nodes. Each eligible return emits one focus command and an actual Focus event closes the phase. No durable Send or external write ran.

The redundant Model return bool and two forwarding action wrappers were removed; the existing request-route guard supplies the same return decision, and bound action arguments supply the same option/runtime calls. The actual Model mutation response, native Back projection, New Task's separate textarea implementation, public UIKit presentation, hardware/software keyboard delivery and source animation timing still need their respective integrated checks. ## Bounded iPad focus return (GAP 010)

[observed] The pinned use-thread-settings-sheet-presentation.ts:114–145 tries focus at most 20 times, 50 ms apart, and gives the last attempt a further 50 ms before closing on exhaustion. Its dismissal handler accepts native transition completion or a 300 ms fallback. The app uses that documented fallback, without claiming native-completion acceleration or exact source animation timing.

[observed] Three root tasks now separate the 300 ms first attempt, 50 ms retries and 1 ms invalidation/focus acknowledgement. The pure policy still validates the exact retained route, editor, content owner and captured settings sheet. Each retry uses the current guarded phase; opening a fresh settings session resets its counter. Matching Focus stops future attempts. Unknown or replaced owners and removed or superseded routes cancel the exact host target. After 20 attempts, the final 50 ms window expires before blur cancels any remaining host request and the phase closes. Grouping four existing attribute lines keeps app.contract at 1500 lines; no framework or native focus shim changed.

It verifies 299+1 ms and 49+1 ms boundaries, exactly 20 focus commands and the final grace period, actual-focus acknowledgement, owner/navigation cancellation before the next retry, and reopening with a fresh fallback. All prior retained-route, stale-callback, unknown-owner and unavailable-editor cases still pass. The module registers each request before scheduling disk work. The existing queued-edit coordinator lock protects admission, editor holds and accepted attachment owners. One serial worker writes files outside that lock, allowing another enqueue or editor hold to arrive during persistence. Accepted work retains the coordinator through completion, including after its Exact answer ends.

[observed] Enqueue publishes its token immediately; update and removal publish after persistence. Optional revision/token guards retain source post-write compensation. Without a guard, the source can publish an update over a newer optimistic enqueue while that enqueue's later write still owns disk. The native result therefore separates the operation's payload from the current accepted row. It exposes the owner epoch, admission sequence floor, row token and revision. The TS adapter does not guess current state from an old operation's payload.

[observed] [T3MobileOutbox.swift](../examples/t3-code/mobile/modules/apple/T3MobileOutbox.swift) keeps per-message envelopes, previous/proposed owners, exact mutation outcomes and deletion receipts. Native completion owns both preparation and finalization. A rename followed by failed directory sync is uncertain, including when rereading finds a terminal envelope. Recovery must establish durability before confirmation. Interrupted pending markers need an explicit commit or rollback decision. Acknowledging a reply does not complete attachment removal. Partial inventory retains readable siblings and prevents collection of unknown byte owners.

[observed] If B was accepted before A becomes uncertain, native saves B's known-no-write failure and byte receipt beside A's unresolved marker. A failed receipt save leaves B uncertain with its in-memory byte ownership retained. It does not start another replay loop.

[inferred] These receipts handle app-local persistence ambiguity across Exact answer lifetimes. They do not add a second transport or a delivery retry loop. Draft-to-queue transfer must consume the exact enqueue outcome before clearing captured content. Unknown outcomes cannot authorize cleanup or delivery.

[observed] [mobile-outbox.ts](../examples/t3-code/mobile/mobile-outbox.ts) keeps a plain per-client projection. Each operation enters native immediately and awaits only its own call. It retains no shared Promise tail, native handle, timer or subscription. Enqueue alone is optimistic. Native tokens, revisions and admission floors prevent an older answer or load from replacing a later record. Status and read recover exact outcomes through fresh calls; acknowledgment is explicit. No root resource or UI route uses this owner yet.

## Durable draft capture transfer

[observed] Captured enqueue now records the original draft presentation and queue record in the same native envelope. The native owner hashes the versioned draft capture, including its full key, incarnation and revision, but excluding newly allocated task IDs and queue time. Admission checks active claims before accepting replacement IDs. Matching captures recover the original transfer; a conflicting active capture must resolve first. Completed tombstones prevent replay from creating a second task. Known failed captures require explicit release before retry.

[observed] The capture owns its original attachment bytes independently of later queue edits, removal and outcome acknowledgment. Staged attachments must have matching regular UUID-backed files before admission. An existing uploaded reference retains its captured environment association. An incomplete or malformed inventory blocks fresh transfer admission and byte collection. Neither the capture nor its cleanup marker is a shared Pending launch receipt.

[observed] [mobile-outbox-transfer-model.ts](../examples/t3-code/mobile/mobile-outbox-transfer-model.ts) validates the native capture and claim. The existing outbox projection exposes transfer lookup, status, captured enqueue and retirement without a shared promise chain or stored native handle. A duplicate reply can name the original task IDs. It removes only its own provisional row and preserves a previous edited row as uncertain when the reply supplies no current outcome. A later response cannot replace the original capture or revive a retired capture.

[observed] [mobile-outbox-transfer-cleanup.ts](../examples/t3-code/mobile/mobile-outbox-transfer-cleanup.ts) applies cleanup synchronously to the captured key and incarnation. A changed content revision conservatively retains text and attachments. Otherwise only equal captured content is removed, including checks for image/file references in remaining text. New selections, workspace and raw whitespace survive persistence. The cleanup decision and mobileOutboxTransferCompletions marker share the ordinary preference document. Repeating a matching marker never clears subsequent edits. Invalid marker data stays visible and blocks destructive replay.

[observed] Native completion reads and re-syncs the exact preference marker before journaling attachment release and retiring the capture. A failed or missing marker cannot release it. The existing release flushes now keep retry entries unless native explicitly reports removal; retained:true no longer consumes an unfinished cleanup. These are app changes and do not alter the desktop client or Exact framework.

[observed] The transfer API and conditional cleanup are now connected to New Task Send. Durable context, uploaded-reference provenance and foreground delivery are described below. Pending editing and recovery routes remain unfinished. Native storage checks do not prove screenshot, motion or performance parity.

## Pending task editor save and restart recovery

[observed] [mobile-pending-task-draft.ts](../examples/t3-code/mobile/mobile-pending-task-draft.ts) adopts a pending creation into the existing draft owners once, preserving original IDs, captured project metadata, ordered attachments, context, settings and workspace. Reopening retains newer or emptied content. Recapture uses the original queue identity and validates uploaded attachment provenance. Conditional cleanup compares actual content and projects existing byte-release intents; it creates no second draft store.

[observed] [mobile-pending-task-editor.ts](../examples/t3-code/mobile/mobile-pending-task-editor.ts) implements awaited Open, Save and Finish operations. Open acquires the persisted editor session's native hold and saves actual content before admitting edits. Save persists the complete immutable update request before each native admission, including retries after a failed preference write. Unknown outcomes retain that request. A committed receipt updates the baseline durably before acknowledgment, while later typing survives. Finish requires an unbound editor whose exact content matches the committed queue baseline. Its temporary plain-data projection is applied by the existing preference writer to concurrent writes; draft and marker remain live until native hold release succeeds. Failed writes or releases retain ownership for retry. A let-go answer does not revive expired handles; after durable cleanup the unchanged captured content remains in the queue, and a later open reconciles remaining live ownership.

[observed] The app-native outbox's resumeUpdate operation accepts the saved original mutation identity under its exact current editor hold, including a prior process epoch or an advanced sequence floor. It verifies the whole original request, fingerprint and baseline before recovering or admitting it. Pending and terminal replacement uncertainty use the existing envelopes and coordinator mutex. No new store, framework protocol or ordinary mutation compatibility path was introduced. Actual Swift coordinator tests pass 40 checks across cold and warm recovery, hold refusal, fingerprint mismatch, stale winners, write failures and cross-row identity collisions.

[observed] [mobile-pending-task-receipts.ts](../examples/t3-code/mobile/mobile-pending-task-receipts.ts) discovers the original final, settings and inline receipts under the editor hold. It recovers exact durability and retires only never-issued reservations. Already attempted commands require explicit preserved-draft recovery; edited content never silently replaces their payload. The driver also checks editor ownership before each unsafe native request, including an editor opened while delivery was awaiting another answer. Known final ACK local cleanup remains eligible.

retains pinned color flattening so native chip roles receive opaque colors over
