185 assertions; layout and existing NewTask checks pass 53 tests and 402 assertions.
No integrated rich chip pixel comparison is claimed.

[observed] An isolated compiled Contract/Runner witness confirms that ready ordinary
mutations call their data source before dependent resources settle, even when a
later mutation supersedes persistence. It also confirms the PR timer's query/mount
identity preserves its deadline across a request-scope change. Its injected data
source is not the new TypeScript runtime; final root integration still needs its
own acceptance. The witness passed its third round after two fixture corrections.


[observed] The inactive [editor runtime](../examples/t3-code/mobile/composer-editor-runtime.ts)
and [ordinary document owner](../examples/t3-code/mobile/composer-editor-owner.ts)
join event admission, draft reduction, command effects and query scheduling. The
ordinary draft hook observes the shared reducer's actual write before persistence.
Retained submit actions include document revisions so later typing, including text
ABA, invalidates them. Duplicate terminal replies remain redundant after later
commands complete. Query timers retain their own identity across scope changes;
immediate work uses a captured root-to-wall-clock offset. The final focused round
passes 61 tests and 246 assertions with strict TypeScript. The combined mobile
suite passes 1,925 tests and 12,630 assertions across 148 files, excluding the
unchanged paused favicon-root test. These results do not activate the new editor.
Voice, Review, Terminal, attachment changes, Send retirement and rich clipboard
imports still need explicit document ownership before Thread migration.


[observed] The subsequent native paste-observation and bundled-icon increment
passes 79 actual UIKit checks on its first round, with 32 operation and 37 protocol
checks still passing. Each intercepted paste embeds the identical full foundation
selection event, including any pending command outcome, and requires the active
foreground editor. Content-size updates do not advance the document event count.
Restricted bundled Pierre icon identifiers load only the pinned local asset family;
invalid or missing keys do not become network requests. This is isolated native
acceptance. Clipboard byte adoption and rich callback handling remain unconnected.


[observed] Normal application builds with these changes launch on iPhone and iPad.
Both accept and delete a local numeric draft probe. The phone also retains the
probe, caret and keyboard through Model settings and Cancel. The probes were
removed. The previously observed iPad keyboard-return difference was not retested
or resolved by this checkpoint. Existing textarea acceptance does not establish
rich-editor parity.


[observed] The app-local [clipboard boundary](../examples/t3-code/mobile/composer-context-clipboard.ts)
and [record decoder](../examples/t3-code/mobile/composer-context-schema.ts) now follow
the pinned fragment and HTML flavors. The raw 200-record bound precedes individual
record decoding. Decoding trims declared fields, drops malformed records and keeps
valid future payloads. Reidentification rewrites annotation screenshot references;
failed attachment imports leave their references visible while omitting the failed
records. The import coordinator owns callbacks only for its invocation and makes
no cleanup calls after a superseded answer. A successful result transfers staged
file responsibility to its caller. Native asset import and atomic publication are
still required before this can serve the visible editor.

[observed] The second clipboard round passes 14 tests with 90 assertions and strict
TypeScript. An independent source comparison executes the pinned Effect schemas
and unchanged helper bodies: two tests with 1,746 assertions pass. These comparisons
do not execute native file import. The first focused round exposed a fixture that
incorrectly expected hydration to replace a context already edited during the
invocation, plus a TypeScript inference error in the new map result; both are fixed.

[observed] The new named capture/observation functions in
[composer-command-context.ts](../examples/t3-code/mobile/composer-command-context.ts)
allow an accepted unmounted edit to update its own context while another thread is
focused. Capture, named text commit and observation share one synchronous turn.
Guards bind the connection, context incarnation, revision and previous value.
The new [record admission gate](../examples/t3-code/mobile/composer-context-record-admission.ts)
also admits canonical element, annotation and future-kind clipboard records through
existing context projection. It checks declared decoded fields against saved values
without rewriting malformed saved data. Existing producer branches retain their
checks. Its first validation round passes 30 tests with 180 assertions across four
files and strict TypeScript, including annotation screenshot retention and invalid
context refusal before pruning. Record validity alone grants no editor or file ownership.


[observed] [Durable editor persistence](../examples/t3-code/mobile/composer-editor-persistence.ts)
and [Send retirement](../examples/t3-code/mobile/composer-editor-retirement.ts) now
record enrolled ordinary documents and actual outgoing command ownership in the
same preferences snapshot. Document text is reconstructed from that snapshot's
shared draft slot rather than duplicated in metadata. Matching pending commands
supply request bytes; unresolved receipts without a pending command retain their
exact request. Invalid metadata remains raw and unavailable through save/reload.
Unobserved legacy writes mark the document blocked without guessing a revision.

[observed] The production coverage gate remains closed: enrolled documents preserve
text after Send, and unenrolled textarea drafts retain their existing behavior.
This preserves pending/attachment cleanup, so it does not promise that every
attachment behind a retained reference remains available. The new named owner
commit checks for a mounted editor immediately before mutation. Native paste
adoption markers and exact cleanup receipts share the preferences snapshot but
perform no file IO themselves. The second durable round passes 48 tests with 455
assertions and strict TypeScript. Full external producer coverage, native retirement
acceptance and visible rich-editor activation remain separate work.

[observed] The pure [insertion projection](../examples/t3-code/mobile/composer-context-insertion.ts)
follows pinned rich spacing, remembered selection, context dependencies and
attachment retention. Unchanged text uses the captured range, including text ABA;
changed text appends. Rich insertion refuses an over-capacity batch as a whole.
Plain fallback inserts raw text and preserves the source's empty-draft removal
rules. This is separate from attachment append's accepted-prefix policy.
The second focused round passes ten tests with 60 assertions and strict types;
an oracle running pinned helper bodies passes 10,795 comparisons. The first round
found an empty-store normalization mismatch, corrected in the second.

[observed] Ordinary [batch context publication](../examples/t3-code/mobile/composer-command-context.ts)
validates the whole saved payload and incoming records before pruning. A detached,
ordered snapshot of [context history](../examples/t3-code/mobile/mobile-new-task-context.ts)
lets a new annotation retain a screenshot found only in Undo history. Existing
callable history behavior is unchanged. Publication requires a fresh named guard
and changes metadata only; it never restores the import's older text. Every added
record enters Undo history, then live records are refreshed before eviction.
Failed validation or stale ownership leaves metadata and history untouched.

[observed] Valid recovery metadata can exceed the live record bound before
projection. Incoming batches and the final live projection remain bounded; the
byte limit counts serialized records. The new publication path refuses exhausted
safe-integer revisions. These boundaries were identified in the pre-test review.
The second focused round passes 29 tests with 171 assertions, including existing
ordinary and named-context cases, and strict TypeScript. The first run found a
test expectation that confused empty context with absent context; only the test
changed. Pure insertion and metadata acceptance do not retain file bytes, adopt
native paste leases, publish an atomic import snapshot or activate the rich editor.

[observed] The new unmounted transaction in
[composer-editor-owner.ts](../examples/t3-code/mobile/composer-editor-owner.ts)
commits a captured ordinary document's text, selection and context together.
Its internal context helper validates current ownership, raw records, dependency
projection and limits, then constructs a detached history copy before calling the
existing document writer once. After acceptance it installs only prepared values.
A refusal leaves the document, context and Undo order unchanged. A mounted editor
for the same document refuses this path even when its incarnation differs.

[observed] The final focused round passes 32 tests with 225 assertions and strict
TypeScript, including the actual preference writer capturing one text/context
snapshot before its awaited write. The first two rounds corrected new test
fixtures: a missing brace and an empty-versus-absent context expectation. Production
code was unchanged after the pre-test review. This metadata transaction does not
reconcile attachment inventories or retain file bytes. It remains inactive in
Terminal, Review and the rich editor until their complete publication paths exist.

[observed] [Native file holds](../examples/t3-code/mobile/modules/apple/T3MobileComposerFileHolds.swift)
protect already saved ordinary attached files for a registered editor's volatile
Undo history. Acquisition checks the exact saved row and native owner, then the
canonical UUID, regular non-symlink file, size and fingerprint. A caller-known
request UUID makes a lost reply retryable without adding another hold. Changed
or released requests cannot reacquire. Acquisition and both removal paths share
the existing coordinator mutex. Uncertain cleanup registration retains protection;
replay saves the candidate again. Release removes protection without unlinking.
Editor retirement ends its claim before queued work can succeed. Failed retirement
registration keeps unresolved holds until a later explicit drain.

[observed] The actual UIKit fixture passes 66 checks on iPad, including ownership,
numeric admission, file replacement, two holders, reply loss and retirement races.
The whole-module SDK check passes after correcting the GhosttyKit header search
path; production bytes did not change between rounds. Post-write uncertainty is
injected after durable replacement returns, not into the fsync syscall. A fresh
coordinator models loss of process-local history. Saved rows prove environment and
draft membership, not historical server origin. At that native checkpoint, visible
file Undo/reupload and runtime acquisition before editing were not integrated.
The runtime acquisition described below is now exercised with controlled native
replies. Visible native Undo/reupload, unpersisted picker files, images, pasted-text
memory and staged imports remain outside that native acceptance.

[observed] The [file-hold adapter](../examples/t3-code/mobile/composer-file-holds-io.ts)
keeps immutable requests in a plain volatile ledger. Native handles and promises
belong only to the current invocation. Lost replies preserve the exact request;
strict receipt checks bind the answer to its captured owner and file. Let-go errors
propagate unchanged without cleanup through the abandoned handle. Never-dispatched
requests retire locally. Release intent is irreversible, and pending, releasing or
retired holds cannot authorize restoration. The first round passes 37 tests with
134 assertions and strict TypeScript. The adapter supports the current closed
DraftFile schema. Exhausted counters leave unresolved cleanup to actual native
owner teardown. Runtime admission and acquisition were separate work at that
checkpoint; the saved-file runtime below now exercises them. Visible native Undo
acceptance remains separate.

[observed] The [plain file history](../examples/t3-code/mobile/composer-file-history.ts)
refreshes live file metadata in source order while preserving each hold's original
acquire request. Images count toward the source history limit but receive no file
hold. Restored files follow context-record order, retain their local identities,
and clear upload IDs so they can upload again. Missing history reports unavailable
files; missing live holds produces no partial candidate or release. Historical
entries also require the IO owner's current usable bindings. Disposal closes the
history and returns cleanup obligations without performing native IO.

[observed] File-history R1 passes 23 tests with 80 assertions and strict TypeScript;
the executed source-oracle run passes 2,725 checks across 678 restore steps and
13 disposals. A joint fixture uses the actual hold adapter and rejects restoration
after release begins. Review caught and corrected array-to-string status coercion
before this round. Inputs require JSON-normalized rows with absent optional fields
omitted. Publication must check the current owner, target, closed flag and expected
revision before installing a candidate or releasing old holds. The saved-file runtime
below integrates mounted publication and legacy chip mapping. Visible native Undo
and incoming producer ownership remain unfinished.

[observed] [composer-attachment-publication.ts](../examples/t3-code/mobile/composer-attachment-publication.ts)
now prepares ordinary inventory changes from complete raw images, files, mixed
orders and cleanup queues. It preserves foreign rows and metadata, retains images
and non-context files, and queues removed canonical context files in the existing
file release owner. It performs no native deletion. Alternate chip bindings that
the current shared uploader cannot resolve are refused. Its first round passes
55 tests with 91 assertions and strict TypeScript.

[observed] The new semantic insertion API in composer-editor-owner.ts retains
the original enrollment, durable incarnation, saved environment identity and
producer ownership across awaits. It projects the latest valid named state.
Equal text, including text ABA, uses the captured UTF-16 range; changed text
appends. The existing strict revision-based API keeps its prior behavior. The
transaction prepares context, history, attachment inventory, ordering, cleanup
and ledger replacements before one concrete document commit. Post-acceptance
work assigns those prepared values. The second round passes 30 tests with 246
assertions, including unchanged strict transaction regressions, and strict
TypeScript. The first round exposed a target reader that initialized malformed
cleanup metadata. Read-only admission and early raw preflight now preserve it.

[observed] [composer-external-context.ts](../examples/t3-code/mobile/composer-external-context.ts)
connects this transaction to enrolled, unmounted ordinary Terminal and Review
insertions. Neither producer implicitly enrolls an untouched draft. The range
comes from durable remembered selection or the end, not a proven live textarea
caret. Review installs its card and clears the consumed form before saving.
Terminal reports accepted state separately from save failure; root dismisses
only the matching capture visit and prevents a second pending request. Failed
saves retain the accepted edit and cleanup obligations. Abandoned saves preserve
the exact cancellation error. Producer R2 passes 43 tests with 270 assertions
across the new 16 cases and existing Terminal/Review suites. Root strict checking
passes after normal regeneration of source declarations.

[observed] This is ordinary publication without native Undo or incoming bytes.
Review's desktop-style global-ID removal refuses owned drafts until an exact
occurrence inspector is available. Queued edits, New Task, legacy unenrolled
insertion and the shared uploader retain their existing implementations. Mounted
rich publication, full file-bearing Send validation, visible native paste and rich-editor
activation remain unfinished. The producer fixtures use mocked native/storage
ports; they do not establish device caret, Undo, upload or pixel parity.

## Ordinary Send handoff and pending feed

[observed] [thread-send-admission.ts](../examples/t3-code/mobile/thread-send-admission.ts) ports ordinary mobile admission without the desktop sender's prompt normalization. It trims text, retains every attachment and context
record, and permits file-only messages. Only a provider that hides the interaction-mode toggle resets Plan mode; the UI preference alone does not. The offered usage command is classified before the message context-count limit, as
in the pinned composer. Round two passed 15 tests and 114 assertions plus 625 controlled comparisons with the executed pinned source. This pure classification performs no command IO, persistence, enqueue or draft clear.

[observed] The ordinary Send candidate uses a tagged version-2 capture in [thread-send-transfer-model.ts](../examples/t3-code/mobile/thread-send-transfer-model.ts). It records the enrolled document incarnation, revision and
remembered selection, raw text, full context, named image/file inventory and attachment order. The queue payload uses trimmed text and all captured attachments. The completion proof describes exactly the saved document and draft
projection after clearing or preserving it. Validation of this data grants no native admission, byte ownership or durability. The second focused round passed 98 tests and 139 assertions, with strict TypeScript. Capture retains raw
order for change detection; completion describes the effective order actually serialized, with null for an absent row. The existing New Task version-1 capture remains separate.

[observed] [thread-send-handoff-draft.ts](../examples/t3-code/mobile/thread-send-handoff-draft.ts) captures the complete ordinary draft before explicit enrollment. It refuses active rich editing or invalid ownership. A queued
claim clears only its unchanged captured document, context and inventory; newer edits produce a preserve proof. The context and owner reducers prepare the replacement state before one document commit, then update the in-memory
editor record so subsequent plain typing remains tracked. Retrying an applied claim never clears again; it refreshes the proof if newer edits changed the saved projection. The final third round passed 17 tests and 216 assertions,
including actual preference serialization and typing after clear. This synchronous helper trusts no decoder as proof of native admission; its caller must obtain the claim from the existing native transfer journal.

[observed] [thread-outbox.ts](../examples/t3-code/mobile/thread-outbox.ts) presents ordinary queued messages after the thread feed, scoped to their canonical server, environment and thread. A completed delivery remains visible
until the synchronized feed contains its message ID. Retained projections from a replaced connection cannot retire that bridge. Local images use the native canonical-file preview; uploaded images use their server upload ID and an
expiring asset URL. Abandoned preview calls release their own pending cache entry so revisiting the thread can retry.

[observed] The pending-feed focused suite passed 69 tests and 346 assertions in round two. Round one exposed two fixture omissions: canonical-home discovery and deletion revision records. Production code did not change between
those rounds. Strict TypeScript passed. These tests use controlled native/transport replies and do not establish native pending-feed rendering or complete ordinary Send parity. Pending editing, source context and copy
presentation and complete recovery UI acceptance remain unfinished. Actual Send was not yet activated at that standalone checkpoint; native transfer and the later phone acceptance are recorded below.

[observed] [mobile-outbox.ts](../examples/t3-code/mobile/mobile-outbox.ts) keeps one transfer dictionary with separate typed New Task and ordinary projections. Ordinary requests include the canonical server, environment, thread
and draft key. Unknown admission retains the immutable capture and proposed identities. Later replies cannot replace a terminal receipt with an earlier queued state. Its second round passed 372 tests and 2,426 assertions across
the outbox and New Task regressions.

[observed] The existing [native store](../examples/t3-code/mobile/modules/apple/T3MobileOutbox.swift) and [owner](../examples/t3-code/mobile/modules/apple/T3MobileOutboxOwner.swift) admit ordinary captures through the same journal
and attachment ownership checks. They verify the exact saved clear-or-preserve proof under the preference mutex and sync those saved bytes before completing the transfer. Upload confirmation, reservation and retry remain blocked
until completion. Read and lookup withhold a compacted ordinary terminal receipt when its current-owner sync fails; status refuses it. The production-body disk fixture passed 110 checks in round two and the full iOS SDK typecheck
passed. Round one's only failure was a fixture visibility declaration. These checks do not establish device delivery or complete ordinary Send parity.

[observed] [thread-send-transfer.ts](../examples/t3-code/mobile/thread-send-transfer.ts) captures the payload before asynchronous work, persists document enrollment before admission, and persists the concrete draft reduction
before requesting completion. A shared per-draft lease prevents overlapping submissions and recovery releases. Explicit prepared recovery may resolve an incomplete inventory; queued retirement and reported completion require a
complete inventory. The delivery driver also avoids scheduling known unfinished ordinary retirement. The final third round passed 92 tests and 663 assertions across the facade, driver, union and draft helper, with strict
TypeScript. Round one's cold fixture omitted the app's hydration wrapper. The third round added exact durability Retry for a retained queued or failed claim whose fresh native outcome is uncertain and matches the message, mutation
and owner epoch. Commit and rollback cannot use that exception.

[observed] [thread-send-recovery.ts](../examples/t3-code/mobile/thread-send-recovery.ts) binds recovery actions to the exact thread visit, native claim, mutation, outcome and owner epoch. Incomplete inventory permits only a
positively identified interrupted-write choice. It never chooses recovery automatically. A second status check prevents a stale button from changing its meaning. Round two passed 28 tests and 196 assertions plus strict TypeScript;
round one's fixture omitted the native decorated current row. At that standalone checkpoint, actual Send, local commands, recovery UI and device acceptance remained unfinished; later integration is recorded below.

[observed] [thread-send-controller.ts](../examples/t3-code/mobile/thread-send-controller.ts) reads captured mobile preferences and explicit import, paste, voice and upload facts before choosing ordinary transfer or a local
command. It reads the actual provider row for feedback and allows cached model settings for offline admission. Strict raw-store checks precede permissive target helpers. Round two passed 45 tests and 354 assertions across
controller, admission and transfer, plus strict TypeScript. The first round's fixture omitted a native envelope generation.

[observed] [thread-send-local-commands.ts](../examples/t3-code/mobile/thread-send-local-commands.ts) owns ephemeral usage panels and scoped feedback notices. Usage opens only when a report exists, then clears text. Feedback
publishes an uploading notice, clears content, awaits persistence and invokes its captured upload callback. It creates no message or queue transfer. Its first round passed 19 tests and 92 assertions plus strict TypeScript. The
copied pure usage and feedback helpers match the existing desktop bodies; read-only comparison to the mobile pin found no required correction in the activated paths. This is controlled-owner evidence, not a full source oracle or
pixel comparison.

[observed] [thread-send-local-clear.ts](../examples/t3-code/mobile/thread-send-local-clear.ts) supplies that owner's concrete no-attachment clear callback. It validates the captured draft before document enrollment and starts the
actual clear before awaiting persistence. Text clear uses the ordinary input reducer; content clear atomically empties text and context. Failed save preserves the accepted clear and reports the error. Round two passed 10 tests and
61 assertions; strict TypeScript passed in round one, with production unchanged in round two. Two fixture expectations were corrected for actual empty-context representation and one-time hydration. Native local-command acceptance remains unfinished; the later root integration is recorded below.

[observed] [thread-local-dock.ts](../examples/t3-code/mobile/thread-local-dock.ts) projects scoped feedback notices and dense usage accounts into [thread-local-dock.contract](../examples/t3-code/mobile/thread-local-dock.contract).
Reset controls carry exact report/account inputs; the projection grants no permission or spending authority. Round one passed eight tests with 29 assertions and strict TypeScript. The isolated Contract build passed in round three,
after unsupported attributes/root props and an incomplete fixture manifest were corrected. Its empty-data compile fixture proves no runtime interaction or native layout.

[observed] Native scrolling, clipping, focus, Copy ID, reset-credit confirmation and RPC remain unverified. Source enter/exit animation, pressed opacity, selectable descriptions and expanded close-button hit areas are not
reproduced. These leaf components do not complete ordinary Send or application parity.

[observed] [thread-send-root.ts](../examples/t3-code/mobile/thread-send-root.ts) and the root Contract now connect ordinary Send, scoped recovery and local cards. The adapter's second round passed 84 tests and 550 assertions plus strict root TypeScript. Parent Contract validation corrected both New Task shared-button call sites; its next strict check found the adapter's missing brace, fixed in the adapter's accepted round. The final parent round built and launched the normal iPhone app. One actual text Send cleared the draft and produced exactly one authoritative fixture-server message, retained after a cold launch. The disabled provider failed to open and no provider runtime started. The combined mobile suite passed 2,444 tests and 15,402 assertions; all five repository checks passed. Delayed notice guards are source-reviewed and local commands have controlled test evidence; their complete device, iPad, offline/file and interrupted-recovery acceptance remain owed.

## Native paste recovery verification

[observed] The native paste candidate in [T3MobileComposerPasteStore.swift](../examples/t3-code/mobile/modules/apple/T3MobileComposerPasteStore.swift) re-synchronizes recovered canonical bytes and their directory ancestors before
publishing a staged receipt or removing the original source. The first approved extra round missed its intended fault injection because the fixture compared URL directory representations. The user then approved one test-only
round. Its two predicate changes compare standardized filesystem paths; production stayed unchanged. [ComposerPasteUIKitTests.swift](../examples/t3-code/mobile/tests/ComposerPasteUIKitTests.swift) passed 147 checks on the assigned
iPad, including failed replay synchronization, exact file-before-directory ordering, retained source bytes, and reuse of the reserved UUID after successful recovery. Fixture compilation, the full native iOS SDK typecheck and
signing passed. The seven paste production files match round four. Two outbox dependencies changed independently for ordinary Send and were included in the recorded round-five input hashes. This proves the isolated recovery
behavior; it does not activate rich paste in the normal composer or establish full paste UI parity.

[observed] [composer-paste-io.ts](../examples/t3-code/mobile/composer-paste-io.ts) invokes the accepted native
image-lease stage, adopt, discard and retire operations. It validates exact request scope, generation, proof
and partial image results, preserves the caller's immutable operation identity for replay, and retains stale
replies for cleanup without authorizing publication. An abandoned invocation rethrows its cancellation
unchanged and performs no further IO. Its [focused
tests](../examples/t3-code/mobile/composer-paste-io.test.ts) passed 14 cases with 139 assertions in round one,
plus strict production TypeScript. These controlled wire fixtures follow the actual native schema; they do not
replay captured UIKit response values. Durable request intake, serialized lifecycle and atomic mounted
publication remain caller responsibilities. Structured clipboard file imports, large-text byte staging and
visible paste are not activated by this adapter.

[observed] [composer-command-selection.ts](../examples/t3-code/mobile/composer-command-selection.ts)
implements the pinned command-menu Limits callback separately from typed Send. When Limits is offered and the
draft has no attachments, selection removes only the slash trigger and preserves the surrounding text and
UTF-16 caret. Attachment-bearing drafts insert the provider command instead. The [editor
runtime](../examples/t3-code/mobile/composer-editor-runtime.ts) captures the local report action with the
native edit and opens the existing scoped panel only after its matching applied terminal. Rejected edits and
changed model, panel or covered-route ownership do not open an old report; newer typing is preserved.
[thread-send-local-commands.ts](../examples/t3-code/mobile/thread-send-local-commands.ts) opens the menu
report without a second draft clear or a message submission. The final third round passed 62 tests with 268
assertions across four files and strict runtime/transitive TypeScript. The first two failures were fixture
setup: a same-line slash after Unicode text, then an undeclared provider/workspace command. These are
controlled runtime/selection tests, not root mounting or native menu/report acceptance.

[observed] Selection and result-order regressions are in
[composer-command-selection.test.ts](../examples/t3-code/mobile/composer-command-selection.test.ts),
[composer-editor-runtime.test.ts](../examples/t3-code/mobile/composer-editor-runtime.test.ts) and
[thread-send-local-commands.test.ts](../examples/t3-code/mobile/thread-send-local-commands.test.ts). The
captured local action shape is declared in
[composer-editor-owner.ts](../examples/t3-code/mobile/composer-editor-owner.ts).

[observed] [thread-send-mounted.ts](../examples/t3-code/mobile/thread-send-mounted.ts) adds an explicitly
admitted mounted-editor path to the existing ordinary Send facade and journal. It saves an exact preserved
draft proof before requesting a native compare-and-set clear. A current applied terminal publishes text,
context, attachment inventory, order and completion together before persistence; rejection, later input or
changed metadata preserves the latest projection. A lost reply may retry only the retained command on that
mount. Cold recovery preserves the saved draft instead of inferring native application. Completed transfers
revoke the live command’s metadata-retirement authority, and carried focus/submit events retain their normal
root effects.

[observed] The mounted capability’s second round passed 256 tests and 1,255 assertions across ten files, plus
strict TypeScript. Round one exposed a completion-proof mismatch between absent context and an explicit empty
row; round two preserves their actual serialized distinction. The tests use the real client, runtime, facade
and preference serialization with controlled native replies. They do not establish rich root activation,
native mounted Send acceptance, clipboard/Undo integration or complete composer parity.

[observed] The combined mobile suite for these prerequisites passed 2,490 tests and 15,792 assertions across
177 files, excluding the unchanged paused favicon-root regression. Strict root TypeScript and the normal
iPhone rebuild passed. A real ordinary text Send after that rebuild cleared its draft and produced exactly one
new fixture-server message; the prior message remained. Providers were disabled and no provider runtime
started. All recorded mobile inputs stayed unchanged through the drive. This confirms ordinary Send after the
additive changes, not activation or native acceptance of the mounted rich editor.

[observed] [thread-send-mounted-local-clear.ts](../examples/t3-code/mobile/thread-send-mounted-local-clear.ts)
adds an explicit no-attachment callback for typed commands on an admitted rich editor. Usage clears text through
the ordinary reducer; feedback removes the live context envelope only with its exact applied native terminal
and unchanged captured ownership. Rejection or newer text, metadata or attachments prevents producer-specific
content-clear acceptance. Publication precedes ACK and persistence. An invocation-owned result receipt handles
synchronous event-first replies, and a fresh verified preference write is required before successful feedback
upload admission. The original named document, incarnation and transport are checked after saving; later
accepted text and route-only navigation remain allowed. Round two passed 172 tests with 1,002 assertions across
nine files, plus strict TypeScript. Round one's metadata fixture used an unreferenced record that source
projection correctly pruned; round two corrected it and added the post-save ownership guard. These controlled
runtime tests do not activate the rich root or establish native typed-command acceptance.

[observed] [thread-send-root.ts](../examples/t3-code/mobile/thread-send-root.ts) now keeps an accepted feedback
clear bound to its original thread while a save is pending across navigation. Its captured transport and
permission must remain valid before dispatch and reply; destination text and the originating scoped notice
remain separate. Round two passed 76 tests and 519 assertions across four files. The first failure was a fixture
namespace collision with another suite's saved catalog. Tests held actual fixture persistence and verified one
original-thread payload after route departure, plus refusal after permission or transport revocation. No real
feedback upload or native delayed-save acceptance is claimed.


[observed] [composer-file-runtime.ts](../examples/t3-code/mobile/composer-file-runtime.ts) now acquires actual native holds for saved canonical files before the ordinary mounted editor becomes editable. Native ready and file readiness are separate from server connection readiness, so offline drafts remain supported. Preparation retains immutable requests for explicit retry after a lost reply. Unsaved picker rows and private pasted-text files cannot pass saved-file admission.

[observed] The mounted observation path in [composer-command-context.ts](../examples/t3-code/mobile/composer-command-context.ts), called through the current editor owner, prepares text, selection, context history, mixed attachment inventory, order and cleanup candidates before one synchronous document commit. Source history refreshes record recency and retains accepted command metadata for Undo even when newer native text no longer references it. Replaying an already staged native envelope does not write or save again. Existing saved picker tokens derive file context only from actual attached rows; explicit saved records take precedence.

[observed] In controlled runtime tests, Delete and Undo use the accepted file-history projector with currently usable native holds for both live and historical files. Restoration keeps the local canonical ID, follows context order and clears the previous upload binding. Images and unrelated files remain in the live inventory. The source history bound counts images and retains live recovery overflow. Releases follow accepted publication and a fresh successful preference write, then use the existing native guarded cleanup. Root retirement drops only unusable JavaScript bookkeeping after relinquishing the keyed component; it does not assert native destruction or release. Native claim lifetime remains responsible for unresolved byte protection.

[observed] The final combined round passed 2,554 tests with 16,143 assertions across 180 files, excluding the unchanged paused favicon-root target. All 15 new saved-file runtime fixtures passed. Root strict TypeScript passed, and full Contract build/types passed. Round one found an incorrect context-order expectation; round two corrected it but the aggregate stopped on a sibling deferred-query fixture; round three completed successfully. The controlled native boundary checks actual serialized canonical rows and byte inventory through the real IO adapter. Normal native build and visible delete/Undo acceptance are still pending.

[observed] This runtime retains the accepted queued Send and typed local-command publication policies. It does not complete incoming picker/paste protection, private pasted-text ownership, external producer migration or full rich-composer parity. It validates the actual visit, named target and saved environment before invocation. Server disconnection does not disable local draft editing. Native ready and file-owner readiness gate editing. Captured menu IDs and query keys use fresh invocation-owned handles. Only a once-claimed native focus, blur or submit event returns a root effect, including its captured alternate flag. Retiring a keyed port delegates unusable JavaScript bookkeeping to native lifetime without asserting destruction or release.

[observed] Normal native baking rejected ambient clock reads in the first activation round. The root now supplies explicit Contract time and retains the latest observation within one live scope, visit and editor. A stale minute snapshot cannot move time backward after an action. Runtime clock callbacks are required; explicit two-clock conversion remains available only when the caller supplies both clocks. Query settlement uses the latest observed Contract time. Without another observation during slow IO, that time is a lower bound rather than the actual wall-clock settlement time.

## Fork from response

[decision] The app's `thread-fork.ts` adapts pinned `AssistantForkButton` through
the existing shared durable dispatch and root `selected` navigation mutation.
It sends one `thread.fork` with mobile creation source, response source thread
and completed run, allocated command/target IDs, and the selected title plus
` fork`. It leaves shared selection and every unsent draft intact until the
root commits navigation. Known capability evidence comes from the item's
source provider-thread/session relation, never the selected fork's sessions.
Only derived eligibility from previously observed source items is retained;
there is no second projection or transport reducer.

[decision] Each invocation binds origin, environment, generation, project,
thread epoch, route visit/admission epoch and the actual response/run. Leaving
and returning to the same visit invalidates it. Native calls and persistence
check that owner before and after yielding; a fresh authenticated session must
grant orchestration operation. Busy blocks duplicate admission. An uncertain
write keeps the shared journal's original IDs and reconciles through the real
target shell; the controller never retries it automatically.

button, 13pt branch symbol, disabled opacity0.4 and the existing native small
activity indicator while busy. The existing native wait reply supplies its
monotonic instant because data modules cannot read `Date.now()`. The controller
uses the source40ms polling/2000ms deadline and opens only a synchronized shell
in the same project. A missing shell gives the source `Fork created` alert and
reconnect/open-from-list guidance. Fork lifecycle disclosure and copying remain

## Failure work-row copy and selection

[observed] On2026-10-10 the installed source's `remote-command-center` failure
has two separate copy paths. Holding its header copies `Provider error`, the
actual160-character message and full projected item JSON. Holding the message
opens a native Copy menu for that whole paragraph. The first copy's pasted
Search value is1607 characters. Search normalizes newlines, so this witnesses
content rather than raw clipboard byte equality. Source captures in
`simserver-ki6NPY/media` are `457461000-1791619801484.png` for header hold and
`598299000-1791619867623.png` for the paragraph menu. The old clone message has no
Copy menu; `542266000-1791619919565.png` records its unsuccessful hold.

[decision] The app's existing work-log native consumer now also presents
prominent provider and usage failures. The shared root still supplies retry
eligibility, its captured run id and command dispatch. An ordinary header tap
has no command; only an admitted, enabled Retry press emits the existing root
action. No framework or desktop source changes. The scalar projection prepares
source summary/message/full-item copying, preserving source base/failure/retry
field order after native dictionary transport. Warning copy retains the actual
failure while its visible label retains the reset time.

[decision] `T3MobileWorkFailure.swift` takes its preferred width from existing
settled feed geometry, reports preferred size through `events.intrinsicSize`,
and lays out into the CSS-assigned frame. This is not a frame-driven size-report
loop. The existing weak route/feedback manager admits foreground copy and owns
one copied row per visit across fork and failure rows. Owner/content changes
cancel holds and menus; destruction cancels feedback. The paragraph retains
its own native whole-text Copy menu and UTF-8/RTFD representations. Clipboard
and presentation work retain no domain projection or second dispatch owner.

[reference] The pinned mobile global CSS declares error text14/19,
timestamp13/17 and Copied11/14. Its `text-foreground-subtle` has no generated
palette token, leaving React Native's black text default. Its failure heading
uses adaptive rose600/400, whose sRGB values are `#ec003f`/`#ff637e`, separately
from the danger icon. These are source behaviors, including the missing-token
fallback. React Native0.88.0-rc.3 uses
[TextKit measurement](https://github.com/facebook/react-native/blob/v0.88.0-rc.3/packages/react-native/ReactCommon/react/renderer/textlayoutmanager/platform/ios/react/renderer/textlayoutmanager/RCTTextLayoutManager.mm),
[baseline offsets](https://github.com/facebook/react-native/blob/v0.88.0-rc.3/packages/react-native/ReactCommon/react/renderer/textlayoutmanager/platform/ios/react/renderer/textlayoutmanager/RCTAttributedTextUtils.mm)
and a0.001-point
[pixel-rounding epsilon](https://github.com/facebook/react-native/blob/v0.88.0-rc.3/packages/react-native/ReactCommon/react/renderer/textlayoutmanager/TextMeasurementRounding.h).
The app follows these in its native failure consumer; this does not alter
Exact's text layout. React Native's
[default text attributes](https://github.com/facebook/react-native/blob/v0.88.0-rc.3/packages/react-native/ReactCommon/react/renderer/attributedstring/TextAttributes.cpp)
explain the black timestamp.

## Queued notice ownership, 2026-10-10

[observed] An executed actual MobileDraftClient flow reproduces a queued Begin
persistence failure appearing in another thread after normal select-thread and
fresh typing. The immutable mobile [thread-composer-error source](https://github.com/pingdotgg/t3code/blob/365aa87982a4d81cc8e0c085e8d1a40ca7daecdc/apps/mobile/src/state/thread-composer-error.ts)

clearing, and typing/navigation do not dismiss it.

[decision] This separate two-round correction keys queued notices by canonical
origin, environment and thread. Begin, Save, Cancel, Retry, attachment and
recovery paths capture their message/record owner before awaits. Only matching
message success clears a scoped notice. Unattributable journal failures remain
global and take precedence. A complete healthy Refresh retires only its captured
global object token, preserving newer identical-text reports and scoped notices.

Shared desktop source, native journal, Contract and framework are unchanged.

## Authorized dependency-only notice correction, 2026-10-10

[decision] The human selects the reviewed dependency-only correction after the
round3 stop. Move the existing state constructor, five DTOs and eleven pure
state/notice operations into `queued-edit-memory.ts`, with one WeakMap owner.
The lower module has no client singleton, native/storage handle or runtime
dependency on MobileDraftClient. Callers import the new owner directly; editor
and persistence operations remain in their existing upper module. Apply the
saved lifecycle candidate and only this dependency correction in an isolated
worktree, then independently review and run one bounded correction/check.
No additional behavior or fourth notice iteration is authorized by this choice.
Native notice acceptance remains pending.

[observed] The one authorized dependency correction removes the startup cycle:
six fresh import orders construct the actual MobileDraftClient. Its single lower
WeakMap owner, direct callers, affected production TypeScript and staged caps
pass review/checks. The candidate remains rejected. Its full mobile run has2,735
passes and2 failures with17,497 assertions across184 files, excluding only paused
favicon-root; the same sorted root baseline has2,726 passes andzero failures.
An unchanged attachment-owner test also fails alone on the candidate and passes
alone on root. The saved R3's unconditional letGoAware wrapper prevents its
intentional late attachment-removal call from recording deferred file release.
The environment-removal fixture returns an invalid connection status before its
intended later disk failure. Contract and generated types reject `selectable`
and `hitSlop`; app strict TypeScript is consequently blocked. Portable
`user-select=text` is also explicitly refused, so it is not a valid replacement.

[decision] Preserve the rejected patch
`/tmp/t3-notice-dependency-rejected-20261010.patch`, SHA256
`8e270eae1615836e9d1cd33f308ce88d90270b4dfd3cd7ba6eb68fd3775a3c75`,
and `/tmp/t3-notice-dependency-rejection-20261010.json`. No candidate code or
native bake/device check follows. Read-only review narrows another candidate to
removal-only native interception, the actual decoded status fixture and an
explicit presentation choice: supported Contract notice/Dismiss with Copy/hit
area parity deferred, or an app-owned UIKit paragraph and button. New direction
is requested while independent history/framework work continues. This is an app
regression and vocabulary misuse, with no Exact issue or child app PR.

## Bounded lossless saved-history ingestion, 2026-10-11

[observed] The authorized offline experiment verified all 7,020 visible/local
item pairs byte-for-byte before replacing duplicate occurrences with indices.
Independent reconstruction restores every one of the saved 27,460,312 bytes
and SHA-256 ab8e9ed004acc7de81bc6bdf3f6beb05f36f3beedb319e232ef794bc58ebd164.
The current production threadSnapshot accepts the restored shared graph. Its
18,004,614-byte envelope is 17.17054 MiB, still 1,227,398 bytes above the app's
16 MiB guard. The encoder's process peak is 105.36 MiB; Bun/JSC decode/schema
peaks at 134.59 MiB. These are not Hermes heap or native-device measurements.
Release reduces reported heap but does not return RSS to baseline. No leak,
complete cleanup or safe app budget is established. No source or limit changed.
The fixture is the older sequence287765 HTTP response, not the later 40 MiB
subscription snapshot. One experiment, no retry; raw payload stayed private.
Evidence: `/Users/nate/projects/t3-offline-ingestion-20261011/report.md`.

[decision] Duplicate-copy removal alone does not solve full-history ingestion.
A production representation/budget needs new bounded direction. This remains
an app problem, without evidence for an Exact framework issue or PR.

[decision] [Issue480](https://github.com/ccheever/exact2/issues/480) now tracks
the app's lossless large-thread loading decision and acceptance criteria.
It preserves the no-truncation requirement and separates transport, native
retention, bridge transfer and JS/UI memory. It does not authorize a new
production limit or reopen a stopped implementation round.

[observed] The subsequent authorized read-only design separates four costs:
network admission, retained native inbox, native-to-JS transfer and the full
JS graph with derived rows/cache. Existing small bridge pieces still join
into a whole JSON string; changing those pieces alone cannot solve the other
limits. The proposed next offline experiment serializes bounded records on
demand, verifies complete value/order/cursor reconstruction, and measures
actual Hermes where available. It does not raise limits, capture live data,
or establish that later 40/53 MiB projections fit the current heap. No source

## Saved-history liveness audit, 2026-10-11

[observed] The authorized read-only audit uses exact Hermes source objects at
`d412d3bd851278712c20cca25d094e32641a0465`. The archive and installed API
source match that pin; the older source working tree is not used as authority.
Allocated bytes and heap capacity are separate counters. Full collections
finish before the saved samples and their count advances at both cleanup
points. The remaining 22,800,328 allocated bytes cannot be explained solely
as reserved heap capacity.

[observed] Both cleanup samples occur inside the generated program's still
active outer function and its `evaluateJavaScript` call. Hermes scans active
register slots; compiler register release does not itself clear a runtime
slot. Temporary references are therefore a plausible retaining path. The
default compilation is OMax, so an unoptimized-build explanation is wrong.
No sample exists after the function and temporary JSI result are gone but
before runtime destruction. Source inspection does not identify the actual
retaining slot, graph identity or root class.

[decision] Accept the counter and sampling-boundary findings, not a leak
diagnosis or production fix. The audit does not execute the host, compiler,
runtime, replay or device, and does not read the saved payload or private
oracle. No source or limit changes follow. A post-return collection/sample
would require a newly authorized bounded run. Evidence is external
`t3-offline-ingestion-20261011/liveness-audit-r1/`; report SHA-256
`f5b37e53855928042f0e60b45ffbdaf5d3682f92a1557e518e2e6e839638f708`
and manifest `cd587b5e670419b51e00e42da2dfe979dce576438b2f8ca05ef90e3f938d148d`.

element, and UIKit's baseline already hides system Back. This preserves
logical navigation and the existing warm-root URL filter. Split iPad and
follow-on local-depth Back remain outside that painted-item filter.

[review] Five production source findings remain. Ordinary project selection
from a retained chooser still pushes a second Draft; the pinned mobile source
calls its captured setProject and untargeted Back when the current previous
route is Draft. An initial scratch callback captures no draft key, then refuses
if another action creates the first Draft before its late result. The reference
creates and activates a draft for that captured-null callback. Captured Send
parent navigation finds a route ID but reduces it to a URL; a newer containing
route with the same URL can receive its transition. Native storeReady starts
an authentication read with physical=false, allowing cancellation, closure or
expiry to advance its lane before that callback settles. An old completed Send
disposition refuses retirement when a newer receipt owns the same key, holding
later obligations behind it. These are source traces, not device reproductions;
the authentication finding establishes no remote mutation abort or corruption.
